- Date: 2026-09-01
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: changes-requested
- Findings:
  1. **Blocker — lexical-only fallback discards lexical relevance.** The selected Chat configuration sets `W_LEXICAL = 0.0`, while `fuse` retains lexical-only candidates at score zero and breaks those ties by `evidence_id`; `retrieve_ranked` applies that configuration without regard to `semantic_fallback` (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:59-65`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:694-735`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/service.rs:427-456`). Thus model unavailability and the future forced-lexical kill switch produce stable-ID ordering rather than BM25 ordering. The verified main/v1/v2 harnesses require healthy semantic execution, so their green results do not cover this path. Preserve the existing ordered lexical candidates when semantic retrieval is unavailable/forced off, or select and validate a non-zero lexical configuration; do not add a parallel provider-specific ranker.
  2. **Blocker — the FTS freshness patch uses the wrong revision and breaks shared FTS/Chat callers.** All four read builders compare `fts_indexed_revision` to `source_revision`, even though FTS completeness is governed by `fts_projection_revision` and folder-only projection changes deliberately need no semantic content revision (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/fts.rs:782-798`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/fts.rs:811-849`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/fts.rs:858-870`). This can admit stale folder projections or reject a current FTS projection whenever the counters diverge. The unconditional join also invalidates existing minimal-schema callers: the full FTS suite is red 10/28 (`no such table: search_source_state`), and Chat is red 25/41 because its fixture's partial source-state table lacks the newly assumed columns. The affected production callers include legacy Chat/scope resolution (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1131-1238`), MCP search/context (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:136-174`), the public search API, and `RetrievalService`. Fence against `fts_projection_revision`, preserve the repository's supported schema contract (or consistently migrate fixtures), and rerun complete caller suites.
  3. **High — folder-only mutations are incorrectly turned into content changes and can make a large folder operation unbounded.** Assignment, subtree move, and cascade deletion materialize affected meeting IDs and execute one `source_revision` upsert per meeting inside the write transaction (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/folder.rs:14-26`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/folder.rs:191-208`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/folder.rs:249-280`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/folder.rs:334-347`). Folder metadata belongs to `fts_projection_revision`; changing semantic source identity dirties valid vectors, forces fallback/re-embedding, and makes subtree operations O(number of meetings) statements under the transaction. Remove these source-revision bumps and rely on the atomic recursive membership fence plus the existing projection revision. If hydration needs a folder metadata epoch, validate that separately rather than aliasing content revision.
  4. **High — hydration validates stable row IDs, not the ranked content identity/revision, so a post-ranking edit can repurpose stale evidence.** `RankedRetrieval` carries no per-meeting revision/hash from candidate validation, the loader reads whatever revision is current when hydration begins, and transcript/notes/summary plans accept unchanged segment/template IDs without comparing candidate content or the revision ranked (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/service.rs:275-286`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/hydration.rs:741-813`). The final fence only proves that the newly loaded revision remained stable during that load (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/hydration.rs:515-567`). If a transcript row, notes blob, or summary is rewritten after ranking but before loading while its stable identity remains, an old semantic hit can publish unrelated new text as if that new text had matched the query. A concurrent folder rename can similarly publish the pre-rename `folder_name`, because rename advances projection metadata but the final fence checks only `source_revision` (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/folder.rs:109-125`). Carry the ranked source/content identity into hydration and omit mismatches; also fence the folder metadata actually published without reclassifying folder changes as semantic content edits.
  5. **Should-fix — bounded loading regresses existing large saved meetings and the shared worker instead of bounding the requested ranges.** The common loader reads 257 summary rows and 10,001 transcript rows, marks the whole meeting incomplete if either probe truncates, and saved-meeting Chat then hard-fails before using its already bounded FTS hit neighborhoods (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/retrieval.rs:1171-1265`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1055-1068`). The indexing worker also records the source as a failure (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/worker.rs:1010-1035`). Existing meetings above 10,000 non-empty segments or 256 summary-history rows therefore lose saved Chat and semantic indexing. Preserve allocation bounds by querying the latest usable summary, a count, and only requested transcript ranges/pages; do not require complete meeting materialization for a bounded prompt.
  6. **Should-fix — hydration can return content after late cancellation.** Cancellation is checked after the final SQL fence, but potentially large source planning, Unicode scans/truncation, Markdown/source assembly, mutation of the accumulated output, and the final return have no subsequent check (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/hydration.rs:288-305`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/hydration.rs:306-432`). The late-cancellation test covers the SQL fence, not cancellation during CPU-side publication. Check before committing each assembled meeting and immediately before every non-empty return; Task 3.4's stream fence should remain defense in depth.
  7. **Should-fix — corroboration is not volume-immune when reranking is active.** Production scores only the bounded rerank head, but `aggregate_meetings` treats every unscored tail candidate as a rejection and then removes the entire authoritative class if any rejected entry exists (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:818-837`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:910-919`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:1007-1015`). Adding duplicate transcript candidates beyond depth 50 can therefore erase otherwise positive transcript corroboration and change meeting order. The volume/crowding regressions pass `rerank_scores = None`, so they do not exercise production's scored-head/unscored-tail state (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking/tests.rs:828-868`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking/tests.rs:946-1035`). Define bounded missing-score semantics that cannot be changed by duplicate tail volume and add the production-shaped regression.
  8. **Should-fix / scope — Task 3.2 silently starts Task 3.5's release gate with a non-authoritative proxy.** `model_benchmark.rs` now asserts the 900 ms release sub-budget from five loops of solo-pair timing multiplied by depth 50 (`frontend/src-tauri/tests/model_benchmark.rs:3962-3993`). The Sprint 3 contract explicitly leaves release-hardware p95 to Task 3.5 and treats `solo-pair p95 * 50` as diagnostic only; this drive-by assertion can fail on an arbitrary development host while still not measuring 50 complete warmed production-runtime samples. Remove the release assertion from Tasks 3.2/3.3 and leave the existing diagnostic until Task 3.5 performs its owned measurement.
- Verification: `git diff --check` passed (existing CRLF warning only); `cargo check --tests` and `cargo fmt --check` passed. Verified-bundle main evaluation passed 11/11 (133/480 exact-`full_gates_failure`-eligible configurations; winner `k=5,wv=1,wl=0,alpha=1.5,title=0,concept=1,gamma=0`; post-selection critical Recall@3 5/5 and strict target-over-decoy/full gates pass). Verified-bundle independent suite passed 11/11 (v1 48/48 replay; v2 6/6 regression). Ranking passed 27/27, hydration 13/13, and folder 6/6. Full FTS repository suite failed 18/28; Chat failed 16/41. Frontend typecheck/Vitest were not rerun because no frontend source was touched.
- Residual risks: v1 is content-disjoint but not release evidence, v2 is post-hoc development evidence, and no separately authored v3 corpus exists in this worktree; v3 independent validation remains mandatory even after the code defects above are corrected. The green semantic harnesses do not establish lexical-fallback behavior, large saved-meeting compatibility, post-ranking content-identity fencing, or release-hardware p95. No Task 3.4 Chat/stream/settings/migration rollout was found. **Task 3.4 remains blocked** by findings 1-7, the red shared suites, and the separate v3 prerequisite.
- Follow-up tasks created: 3.2.R11 (lexical-fallback ordering, scored-head corroboration semantics, and removal of the premature Task 3.5 assertion); 3.3.R3 (FTS projection fence/backwards compatibility, folder revision/resource correction, ranked-content/folder-metadata fencing, range-bounded saved loading, and final cancellation checks)

### Review R34 — after current uncommitted Sprint 3 Task 3.4
- Date: 2026-09-01
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: changes-requested
- Findings:
  1. **Blocker — the legacy all/meeting streaming command never receives the shared retrieval lifecycle, so broad streaming remains lexical while non-streaming, scoped streaming, and MCP use Fast retrieval.** `api_chat_with_meetings_stream` still calls the compatibility `prepare_chat_inputs`, which deliberately supplies `None` for the lifecycle; the broad branch then logs lifecycle unavailability and takes the old lexical path (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:290-309`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:621-675`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1633-1666`). This directly violates identical streaming/non-streaming evidence and leaves one Tauri surface outside the lifecycle shared by scoped Chat and MCP. Pass the process lifecycle through this command too, and make runtime preparation unable to silently omit it; retain an explicitly named lexical/test helper only where needed.
  2. **Blocker — explicit cancellation still emits a final `chat-stream-done` containing partial answer and sources.** The cancelled LLM branch calls the event helper with `allow_cancelled = true`, and that helper therefore clears ownership and emits despite the cancelled token (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1800-1812`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1891-1915`). Task 3.4 requires explicit user/stream cancellation to emit no final answer, source, or done event. Keep the existing ownership check for stale streams, but return after owner-only cleanup on cancellation rather than finalizing partial state.
  3. **Blocker — the Sprint 2 transitional retired-generation GC alternative was not removed.** GC still accepts either an acknowledged Fast query or `transitional_replacement_serving`, allowing a clean restarted replacement to delete a retired generation with zero acknowledged product queries (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/index.rs:1750-1777`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/index.rs:1786-1817`). Task 3.4 explicitly expires this alternative and requires one acknowledged successful Fast query after restart. Delete the transitional arm/helper and update its regressions so zero acknowledgements always retain the generation.
  4. **High — the mandatory kill-switch persistence contract has no fresh/upgrade/toggle regression, and the getter masks a missing migration while the setter cannot.** The migration adds the persisted default and the setter updates it, but the getter converts a missing column into `false`; in that partial-schema state the read appears healthy and the next toggle fails (`frontend/src-tauri/migrations/20260901000000_add_force_lexical_retrieval.sql:1`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/setting.rs:32-68`). No force-lexical test exists in the settings repository or migration suite, and the focused Chat tests all pass without exercising the new lifecycle branch. Add the required fresh database, pre-existing settings-row upgrade, default-false, round-trip/reopen, and next-request enable/disable coverage; fail closed on real migration errors rather than maintaining split read/write behavior.
  5. **High — MCP parity is wired but unproved, and its manually reconstructed source payload has already drifted from the shared `ChatSource` contract.** MCP correctly clones the process lifecycle and calls shared preparation, but its only three tests cover tool names/JSON serialization, not all-scope Fast preparation or forced lexical behavior (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:17-25`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:177-237`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:367-395`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:415-458`). It also manually emits five source fields and drops `sourceKind`, whereas hydrated Tauri sources populate that field (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:202-214`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1153-1161`). Serialize the shared source value (or one shared conversion) and add one MCP/shared-preparation regression proving the same retained hydrated sources and kill-switch behavior as Tauri.
  6. **Should-fix — forced lexical state is only an ad-hoc log line, not a typed preparation diagnostic that can survive the next Settings/diagnostics task.** Semantic degradation remains typed on `RankedRetrieval`, while forced mode bypasses that result and records only `log::info!` (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:621-675`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/service.rs:275-289`). This makes Sprint 5.3's required user-forced-versus-failure status depend on parsing logs or re-reading settings and invites Tauri/MCP drift. Carry a small shared retrieval-mode/reason value out of preparation (forced lexical, semantic fallback reason, or hybrid) and log/report that value; do not add a second diagnostics service.
- Cheaper alternatives: Keep one Rust preparation function for every runtime caller, require the already-managed `RetrievalLifecycle`, and let only a clearly named test/legacy helper omit it. Reuse the existing stream ownership guard but never set `allow_cancelled` for completion. Serialize `ChatSource` directly in MCP. Remove the transitional GC predicate rather than replacing it. One typed preparation mode is enough for current logs and Sprint 5.3 diagnostics.
- Verification: inspected the full uncommitted diff and traced Tauri streaming/non-streaming, scoped Chat, MCP startup/call preparation, source publication/persistence shape, settings migration/repository, hydration scope/cancellation fences, and retired-generation acknowledgement/GC. `git diff --check` ✅ (existing CRLF warning only); focused Chat tests ✅ 41/41; focused MCP tests ✅ 3/3. Per instruction, no production model, bundle, or evaluation command was run.
- Residual risks: lifecycle reuse is in the correct Rust layer and MCP does not construct a parallel worker; folder descendants and today allow-lists still pass through the authoritative scope/hydration fences. Existing source rows remain backward-readable because the added `sourceKind` field is optional. Release acceptance remains independently blocked: the user waiver covered only running a production test, not the separately authored corpus gate, and the recorded v3/v4/v5 production evidence remains failing/blocked (`docs/notes-chat-improvement-execution.md:1763-1771`, `docs/notes-chat-improvement-execution.md:1803-1811`, `docs/notes-chat-improvement-execution.md:1823-1831`).

### Review R35 — Sprint 3 Task 3.4 after R34 remediation
- Date: 2026-09-01
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: changes-requested
- Findings:
  1. **High — the required restart persistence regression is still absent.** The new migration test proves fresh/default, same-connection enable/disable, upgrade of a populated settings row, and missing-column failure, but both databases are in-memory pools and neither is closed and reopened; the test named `defaults_persists_and_survives_upgrade` therefore cannot catch a setter that appears to toggle in-process but fails to survive application restart (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/migration_tests.rs:12-21`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/migration_tests.rs:23-77`). There is also no settings-repository test module around the getter/setter (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/setting.rs:31-50`). This leaves an explicit Task 3.4 acceptance behavior unguarded and directly constrains Sprint 5.3's Settings control, which must trust durable next-request enable/disable state (`docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-3-broad-hybrid-chat.md:476-486`, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-3-broad-hybrid-chat.md:507-513`). Use one temporary file-backed SQLite database, migrate/set/close/reopen/read, and keep the existing focused migration assertions.
  2. **High — R34's MCP and cancellation regressions were not added, so two remediated product boundaries remain deletion-proof only by review.** MCP now correctly serializes the shared `ChatSource`, preserving optional `sourceKind`, but its suite is still only three tool-list/JSON-RPC envelope tests and never invokes shared preparation, forced lexical mode, or source serialization (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:190-207`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:408-451`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:218-229`). Likewise, the cancellation suite checks token selection/ownership only; it never exercises `stream_chat` or asserts that cancellation emits no final/source/done event (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1797-1851`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:3192-3240`). This is the parity/effect coverage R34 explicitly requested and will otherwise drift when Sprint 5.3 exposes retrieval diagnostics and when later Chat persistence/source work changes event payloads. Add one shared-preparation/MCP serialization test and one event-observing cancelled-stream test; no new MCP or event infrastructure is needed.
  3. **Medium — runtime lifecycle availability remains optional at the MCP/shared API boundary.** All four Tauri commands now obtain managed `RetrievalLifecycle` state and production MCP startup currently clones that same state, so the original missed legacy streaming caller is functionally repaired (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1524-1555`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1589-1623`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1648-1726`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:724-745`). However, shared broad preparation and MCP still accept `Option<RetrievalLifecycle>`; `spawn_from_app` will start MCP with `None` if retrieval state is absent, silently selecting `LifecycleUnavailable` lexical preparation (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:321-344`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:591-597`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:177-200`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:360-388`). R34 asked that runtime preparation be unable to omit the managed lifecycle. Make the production/shared entry point require `RetrievalLifecycle`; keep a clearly test-only or explicitly lexical helper for tests that need no lifecycle. This avoids constraining Sprint 4 deep saved scopes and Sprint 5.3 diagnostics with a second accidental fallback mode.
- Cheaper alternatives: One file-backed repository persistence test closes finding 1. For finding 2, test the existing shared serializer/preparation and stream event guard directly rather than constructing a second MCP retrieval path. For finding 3, remove `Option` from the runtime entry point and preserve only one explicitly named no-lifecycle test helper.
- Verification: Re-read R34/remediation, Task 3.4's contract, and the remediated Chat, MCP, settings/migration, retrieval-service, and index-GC surfaces. Focused suites passed: Chat 41/41, MCP 3/3, migration 8/8, retrieval index 59/59; `git diff --check` passed with the existing ROADMAP CRLF warning. No model, bundle, or production-evaluation command was run.
- Residual risks: The implementation itself now suppresses completion after cancellation (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1797-1824`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1900-1924`), removes transitional GC eligibility and requires a nonzero successful-Fast acknowledgement (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/index.rs:1750-1784`), directly serializes MCP `ChatSource`, and carries distinct typed `ForcedLexical` versus `SemanticFallback` preparation states (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:258-264`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:588-676`). Those four mechanisms are structurally sound, but the missing required regressions and still-optional runtime lifecycle prevent approval. Independent Task 3.2 corpus/release acceptance remains blocked and was not re-evaluated here.

### Review R40 — pre-Sprint-4 architecture/documentation review
- Date: 2026-09-02
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: changes-requested
- Findings (ordered by impact):
  1. **Blocker — the Sprint 4/5 records do not carry the still-open Sprint 3 release gates explicitly enough to prevent later closure from laundering rejected evidence.** Sprint 4 is still described as wholly blocked on Sprint 3 completion, but its hardening and sprint-close criteria merely say to run the evaluation/native checks; Sprint 5 similarly delegates to broad “all architecture gates” wording (`docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:3-9`, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:423-436`, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:470-483`, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:563-574`; `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-5-search-release.md:427-490`, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-5-search-release.md:512-526`). At the reviewed pushed head, Sprint 3 implementation and its production-rerank guard are present, but release acceptance remains blocked on a valid independently authored Portuguese corpus, production-path quality and provider-answer evidence, native/R13 evidence, and exact-head CI (`docs/notes-chat-improvement-execution.md:1923-1933`, `docs/notes-chat-improvement-execution.md:1942-1957`). **Minimal update:** split “implementation dependency” from “release/close dependency”: record commits `62d7730` and `1047367` as the reviewed implementation baseline; permit sequenced Sprint 4 implementation after these documentation corrections and per-task human approval; make Task 4.5, Sprint 4 close, Task 5.5, and release close inherit each named open gate unchanged. State directly that V1-V10 and the currently rejected corpus fixtures/harnesses are not acceptance evidence, internal production testing without a corpus is diagnostic only, and no later Fast/Deep result can bypass the missing independently authored corpus or provider-answer evidence.
  2. **High — mode compatibility is ambiguous and can turn legacy/MCP calls into paid Deep calls.** The plan allows an unknown mode either to fail or use an unspecified “safe default,” while separately requiring MCP to remain Fast-only (`docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:124-141`, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:154-172`). The current shared MCP path calls the same Rust preparation function as Tauri and has no mode field (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:187-240`), while new interactive UI alone is meant to default Deep. **Minimal update:** define compatibility deterministically: the interactive frontend explicitly sends `deep` for a new session; omitted mode on every pre-existing/legacy/non-interactive/MCP contract resolves to `fast`; an explicit unknown value is rejected; live ignores no silently accepted value because the UI sends Fast/no-Deep there. Require an MCP behavioral regression through shared preparation. Also correct the stale architecture statement that Fast makes no pre-answer Chat-model call: follow-up rewrite already calls the configured provider (`docs/hybrid-rag/architecture.md:1399-1413`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:530-559`); Fast means no **Deep planner** call, not universally one provider round-trip.
  3. **High — the plans invite parallel cancellation registries and unfenced progress publication instead of extending the accepted Sprint 3 ownership boundary.** Task 4.1 says to add a non-streaming token registry, Task 5.2 asks for another request-ID cancellation mechanism, and Deep progress is only said to use the existing event channel (`docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:134-150`; `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-5-search-release.md:216-235`). Sprint 3 already has Rust-owned stream claiming/replacement and one atomic cancellation/ownership publication guard (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:286-295`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1665-1730`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1871-1962`). **Minimal update:** require one small Rust request-ownership/cancellation mechanism, generalized from or sharing the semantics of the existing guard and keyed so Chat and sidebar may coexist; 4.1 establishes it and 5.2 reuses it. Require terminal/error/timeout cleanup and bounded registry lifetime. Deep progress must be emitted by the Chat adapter through that same stream-ID ownership/cancellation publication fence; `retrieval/agent.rs` may accept a privacy-safe progress callback/sink but must not own `AppHandle`, Tauri events, or a second event bus. Add stale/replaced/cancelled-progress tests. This also preserves the cancellation/source publication fence that Sprint 5 deletion work stacks on.
  4. **High — the proposed 512-token/15-second planner contract is not implementable by blindly reusing the current seven-provider call as documented.** Task 4.2 correctly asks for the existing shared LLM client, but assumes provider-independent output and timeout ceilings (`docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:195-220`, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:227-276`). The shared client covers seven providers, but currently forwards `max_tokens` only to Custom OpenAI, hardcodes Claude to 2048, and gives the underlying request a 300-second timeout (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:73-114`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:135-190`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:320-356`). An outer timeout alone also does not document cancellation of queued/local model work. **Minimal update:** require a planner-specific bounded generation options path inside the existing shared client/request builder—no second provider client—with a hard response-byte/parser cap for every provider, provider-specific max-output support where available, a scoped child cancellation token cancelled on per-call/total deadline, and a provider capability/fallback matrix including BuiltInAI/Ollama/Custom OpenAI. If a provider cannot enforce the requested generation bound, fail to current Fast evidence; do not weaken the bound or fork provider logic into `retrieval/agent.rs`.
  5. **High — the forced-lexical rollback is not carried through Deep and the future search/MCP contracts.** Sprint 3 now reads the persisted switch on every shared Chat preparation and reports forced lexical distinctly (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:592-601`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:641-714`), and architecture requires every retrieval surface to use the existing lexical fallback (`docs/hybrid-rag/architecture.md:1694-1711`). Sprint 4's Deep loop and cross-scope acceptance never explicitly test it, while Sprint 5 mentions the UI and final matrix but not ownership of the shared decision in Tasks 5.1/5.2 (`docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:203-261`, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:402-453`; `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-5-search-release.md:134-183`, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-5-search-release.md:216-253`). **Minimal update:** state that the one persisted setting is read at the shared Rust preparation/service boundary; every initial/additional Deep search and every Tauri/MCP/sidebar hybrid request must select the existing lexical fallback and preserve the typed `ForcedLexical` reason. Add Fast/Deep and all-surface enable-next-request, restart, and disable-restore checks; do not add a second setting or diagnostics service.
  6. **Medium — Deep action authority and MCP timeout teardown need tighter bounds before implementation.** Architecture says Deep may open an “authorized candidate meeting,” but Sprint 4 only clearly forbids IDs outside the broad scope; in `All`, a model-invented existing ID is therefore scope-valid even when it was never offered as a capability (`docs/hybrid-rag/architecture.md:1430-1462`; `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:241-252`). Sprint 5 promises an MCP server timeout while disclaiming client cancellation, without requiring that timeout to cancel queued/running retrieval (`docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-5-search-release.md:227-252`; `docs/hybrid-rag/architecture.md:1579-1588`). **Minimal update:** validate `openMeetingIds` against the bounded candidate/card IDs actually supplied to that planner round and `expandEvidenceIds` against currently retained/known evidence IDs, in addition to revalidating authoritative scope. Give MCP requests an internal deadline-owned cancellation token passed through the shared service even though no public MCP cancel method is claimed, so timeout releases scheduler/ONNX work rather than merely dropping result publication.
- Cheaper alternatives: Keep Deep orchestration in Rust as the thin `retrieval/agent.rs` policy already planned, but reuse `RetrievalService`, authoritative hydration, the shared seven-provider client, the existing Chat ownership/event fence, and the persisted settings repository. Extend those seams with mode/options/progress inputs rather than creating a planner HTTP client, frontend planner, second event bus, separate per-surface kill switch, or unrelated cancellation registries. Documentation-only line updates are sufficient before implementation; no Sprint 4/5 source design rewrite is needed.
- Start/close decision: **Yes, Sprint 4 implementation may start after the above documentation edits, in dependency order beginning with 4.1 and only after the existing per-task human approval.** The pushed Sprint 3 mechanism is an adequate implementation baseline. Sprint 3 release acceptance remains open; Task 4.5 cannot claim quality completion, Sprint 4 cannot close, Sprint 5 release qualification cannot pass, and no release-readiness claim is permitted until every carried gate above has valid evidence.
- Verification: Read-only review of pushed commits `62d7730` and `1047367`, current `docs/hybrid-rag/architecture.md`, both Sprint 4/5 PRDs, shared Tauri/MCP Chat preparation, stream cancellation/publication, persisted kill-switch flow, and the shared seven-provider client. The rejected corpus fixtures/harnesses and their local modifications were not read, run, reviewed, or treated as evidence. No source or Sprint 4/5 document was changed.
- Follow-up tasks created: documentation-only amendments to the Sprint 4 PRD, Sprint 5 PRD, and `docs/hybrid-rag/architecture.md`; no implementation task authorized by this review.

### Review R43 — after Task HR-4.2
- Date: 2026-09-02
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: changes-requested
- Findings (ordered by impact):
  1. **Blocker — final authority checks fail open, and the capability allow-list is not the list actually published to the planner.** The prompt can stop adding cards/evidence at its character check, but admission later reconstructs `card_ids` from every ranked item rather than recording only cards that were actually written; expansion similarly admits every hydrated retained ID, including IDs omitted from a truncated planner prompt (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:480-490`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:540-551`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:976-1004`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:909-919`). More importantly, a failed final scope read or failed re-hydration after membership shrinks explicitly keeps the previous context and then publishes it (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:790-828`). The agent also retains `input.scope` for later actions/revalidation instead of adopting the service's normalized authoritative `ranked.scope`, so an `All` request narrowed by a `folder:"..."` operator is not revalidated against the boundary the initial service actually resolved (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:423-440`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:575-584`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:698-715`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/service.rs:520-544`). Final authority failure must suppress/fail the final publication, not retain stale evidence; capability IDs must be captured while the bounded prompt is built; every later operation must use the resolved original scope. This is directly on Task 4.4's frozen snapshot/today membership path and Task 4.5's deletion race, so neither can safely stack on the current seam.
  2. **High — cancellation is not reliably typed, and the claimed seven-provider deadline/cancellation contract does not cancel all underlying work.** Initial retrieval and hydration map every error, including `RetrievalError::Cancelled`, to `DeepPreparationError::InitialRetrieval` (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:433-442`). The required focused test failed in this review because cancellation during hydration surfaced as `InitialRetrieval(Database("... retrieval cancelled"))`, not `Cancelled`; the hydration adapter's exact string comparison does not match SQLx's wrapped error text (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/hydration.rs:168-177`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:884-913`). For remote providers, the child token is selected only until response headers; body reads do not select cancellation (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:364-398`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:422-448`). BuiltInAI receives no bounded-client deadline and reads an unbounded sidecar line before checking the 8 KiB cap (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:318-337`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/sidecar.rs:457-474`). On the agent timeout path, the generation future has already been dropped before `child.cancel()` runs, so the BuiltInAI future is no longer present to observe cancellation and shut down the sidecar (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:498-525`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/client.rs:211-224`). The capability matrix simply declares all providers fully supported and tests construction/pre-cancellation, not in-flight body/sidecar cancellation (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:272-289`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:913-930`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:1033-1076`). This can leave local generation running after timeout and contend with existing summary/final-answer callers of the one global sidecar.
  3. **High — `openMeetingIds` and `expandEvidenceIds` do not implement their advertised evidence operations.** Opening a meeting performs the same original-query retrieval inside a one-ID scope, so it generally returns the query-matching evidence that made the meeting a card rather than opening deeper meeting evidence; expansion discards the selected evidence IDs and merely reruns that same query against their owner meetings (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:605-644`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:645-688`). The open test does not prove an open added evidence: `m-b` already matches the initial `zulu planning` query and the assertion only checks that it remains present (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:332-378`). The expansion test likewise checks scope exclusion, not neighborhood/deeper evidence (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:441-474`). This is a false extension point for Task 4.3's authoritative saved-meeting context and Task 4.4's bounded per-meeting coverage; those tasks would have to replace, rather than extend, these actions.
  4. **High — neither the 24,000-character input limit nor the 30-second total preparation limit is hard.** The prompt writes an unbounded question/effective-query/prior-action header before any cap check, and after evidence reaches the cap it unconditionally appends the truncation notice, coverage heading, and final instruction (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:946-970`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:983-1022`). Meeting titles, IDs, and evidence are also interpolated without escaping; a title is outside the `<evidence>` boundary and evidence can inject a closing tag (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:972-992`). The time budget is checked only before operations: initial retrieval/hydration have no total-deadline wrapper, and any additional retrieval/rank/hydration begun just before expiry may run past 30 seconds (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:421-470`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:556-688`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:725-768`). The execution record itself concedes operation-boundary enforcement while claiming the numeric contract complete (`docs/notes-chat-improvement-execution.md:208-210`). This undermines Deep-as-default latency and will be amplified by Task 4.4's 100-meeting scope.
  5. **High — mixed-success rounds lose the existing fusion/fallback semantics.** One additional retrieval's semantic fallback changes the whole accumulated pool to `LexicalOnly`, including healthy semantic candidates from the initial pass; conversely, a later successful semantic search cannot restore Hybrid after an earlier fallback (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:587-590`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:627-630`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:673-677`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:720-744`). All arbitrary planner queries are also folded into the existing single `Rewritten` provenance/rank namespace, whose fusion intentionally keeps only each candidate's best rank per channel (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:856-879`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/service.rs:701-711`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:664-715`). The result is not a defined fusion of independent additional searches and can reorder/drop semantic value unpredictably. Task 4.4's query-aware broad coverage cannot build safely on this global fallback bit.
  6. **Medium — progress and MCP evidence are weaker than the recorded contract.** `InitialRetrieval` is emitted only after retrieval/hydration finishes, and one `AdditionalSearch` event is emitted only after all query/open/expand operations, rather than at each phase/search boundary (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:423-450`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:562-577`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:769-776`). The Chat adapter correctly carries only stage/count data through the existing ownership fence and the frontend filters by stream identity (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:2318-2375`; `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx:276-281`, `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx:635-664`), but users can still see only the generic pre-existing spinner during the longest initial stage. MCP correctly rejects explicit Deep before shared preparation (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:216-250`), yet its behavioral omission test runs with forced lexical enabled, so it would still pass if the ordinary omitted-mode default accidentally became Deep (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:495-528`). MCP final generation remains the pre-existing duplicated, uncancelled path and must not be mistaken for shared Deep parity (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:187-213`).
- Validated structural choices: orchestration is in the Rust retrieval layer; it reuses `RetrievalService`, production ranking/hydration, the shared LLM request builder, persisted force-lexical setting, and the existing Chat ownership/Tauri event fence. No second runtime, HTTP client abstraction, event bus, cancellation registry, persistence field, or raw planner/query/evidence event was added. Fast and forced-lexical paths do not construct the planner or emit Deep progress (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:777-794`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:834-920`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:3428-3467`). Provider round trips are counted through rewrite plus planner and logged with final generation (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:713-744`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:903-909`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:2201-2206`). These good layer choices do not close the authority, bounds, cancellation, and action-semantics failures above.
- Cheaper alternatives: keep the current thin Rust agent and existing infrastructure, but (a) create one total-deadline child token/timer and pass it through retrieval, ranking, hydration, and bounded generation instead of elapsed-time checks plus an outer planner timeout; (b) have the bounded prompt builder return exactly the card/evidence capability sets it emitted and serialize/escape every untrusted field; (c) use the initial service's resolved scope for all later work and fail closed on final revalidation/rehydration errors; (d) either implement open/expand with existing authoritative source/range hydration or remove those ineffective actions until that behavior exists; and (e) derive ranking mode from actual accumulated provenance rather than one sticky fallback flag. No new client/runtime/event infrastructure is needed.
- Acceptance/proceed decision: **Task 4.2 may not be accepted. Task 4.4 may not proceed because it depends on 4.2. Task 4.3 is dependency-independent in the PRD, but should not be stacked onto this unaccepted shared `api/chat.rs`/retrieval worktree; only an isolated disjoint branch would be reasonable before R43 remediation.** No release-readiness conclusion is made; all inherited Sprint 3 gates remain open.
- Verification: read-only review of all Task 4.2 production/test/doc changes; rejected corpus fixtures and `retrieval_evaluation*` harnesses were neither read nor used as evidence. `cargo test --lib retrieval::agent::tests` **failed 1/20** at `cancellation_during_planner_call_aborts_without_answer_handoff`; therefore the recorded 20/20 and 739-test success cannot be accepted as current evidence. `cargo test --lib summary::llm_client::tests` passed 18/18; `cargo test --lib api::chat::tests` passed 50/50; `cargo test --lib mcp::server::tests` passed 5/5; frontend typecheck and focused Chat scope Vitest passed 18/18 (with existing React `act(...)` warnings).
- Follow-up tasks created: HR-4.2.R43 — fail-closed authority/capability remediation, real open/expand semantics, hard input/total-time/provider cancellation bounds, mixed-round fusion correction, progress/MCP regressions, and corrected verification record.

### Review R44 — after Task HR-4.2.R43
- Date: 2026-09-02
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: changes-requested
- Findings (ordered by impact):
  1. **Blocker — the final authority fence still publishes stale All-scope evidence and can publish after the total budget expires.** The adopted normalized scope is now correctly retained, but `revalidate_ids_in_scope` resolves `All` to the permissive `ScopeFilter::All` and therefore does not establish current existence or source revision (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/service.rs:480-499`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/service.rs:607`). The final fence re-hydrates only when that membership call reports fewer IDs; unchanged membership takes `Ok(_) => {}` and returns the previously hydrated context (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:866-925`). Consequently, a meeting deleted in `All`, or content changed after the prior hydration, can still publish. The new deletion test does not exercise its comment's race: its shared `on_call` hook runs on the first planner call, before the additional search can add `m-b`, rather than only immediately before round two (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:78-105`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:1216-1254`). The same `Ok(_)` path checks only the parent token before answer handoff, not the cancelled budget token, so expiry during final validation can still succeed (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:927-948`). This remains directly unsafe for Task 4.4 frozen/today membership and Task 4.5 deletion/source scrubbing.
  2. **High — the complete 24k prompt cap and delimiter boundary are still not hard.** Question/query/title/text are bounded or encoded, but prior-action IDs are unbounded, and meeting IDs, evidence IDs, and source kinds are interpolated raw into card lines/XML-like attributes (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1219-1245`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1272-1301`). Ten long capability IDs admitted in round one can make round two's header alone consume the cap; `saturating_sub` then budgets zero evidence but does not truncate the already oversized header, the suffix is still appended, and only a debug assertion notices (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1261-1267`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1303-1314`). Raw quote/newline/tag characters in authoritative IDs can also forge attributes/card lines. The tests cover long evidence text and malicious titles, not long/injecting identifiers or the complete round-two prompt (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:707-742`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:1258-1291`). Exact emitted capability capture is improved, but the bounded capability surface is not yet closed.
  3. **High — cancellation/deadline closure is incomplete outside the remote HTTP body.** `initial_failure` still does not map the typed `RetrievalError::Cancelled` variant itself; it returns `InitialRetrieval(Cancelled)` whenever neither sampled token is currently cancelled, contrary to the R43 requirement that retrieval/hydration cancellation be typed by variant (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:957-971`). The 30-second watchdog only cancels a token: authoritative SQL loads check it before and after an awaited database call rather than racing that call, so live DB work can overrun the deadline (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/retrieval.rs:1403-1412`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:716-736`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:754-774`). BuiltInAI likewise waits for the global manager lock and `ensure_running` before entering the cancellation-select that shuts down generation, so sidecar startup/model switching can remain live past the 15/30-second child deadline (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/client.rs:162-181`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/client.rs:206-228`). Remote response-body cancellation and its 8 KiB cap are now real (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:363-411`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:418-446`), and the sidecar line has a 1 MiB allocation cap plus restart-on-read-error (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/sidecar.rs:15-39`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/sidecar.rs:466-493`), but those do not make the whole preparation/provider lifecycle deadline-hard.
  4. **High — planner query slots are stored but still do not implement independent-query fusion.** `merge_candidates` correctly preserves distinct provenance records, but `channel_positions` deliberately takes only the minimum key per candidate (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1130-1155`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:670-689`). `fuse` then concatenates slots into one semantic and one lexical list and contributes each evidence ID only once per channel; repeated support from separate planner searches receives no additional fusion contribution, and earlier slots dominate every later slot regardless of each query's own rank (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:692-759`). The new tests assert only that slot numbers survive and decide a tie, not that independently ranked query lists merge with defined cross-query semantics (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:744-779`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking/tests.rs:186-198`). Task 4.4's query-aware broad coverage would therefore inherit the same undefined/biased multi-query ranking that R43 asked to remove.
  5. **Medium — open does not publish the complete authoritative head it claims, and same-meeting open/expand operations silently collapse.** The existing loader returns latest summary, notes, and transcript head/neighborhood (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/retrieval.rs:195-212`), but `append_authoritative_evidence` appends only up to eight transcript segments; it creates no summary/note ranked identities (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1037-1093`). In addition, `loaded_meetings` keeps only the first loaded source for a meeting, so an open and expansion (or multiple expansion groups) for that meeting do not merge their requested ranges (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:694-700`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:701-776`). Current tests prove one unmatched transcript becomes visible, but not authoritative summary/note head parity or combined open/expand coverage (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:335-405`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:501-554`). This constrains Task 4.3's mandatory summary/notes invariants and Task 4.4's bounded per-meeting coverage.
- Validated closures / structural choices: orchestration remains in the Rust retrieval layer and reuses `RetrievalService`, production ranking/hydration, the shared seven-provider client, the existing repository loader, Chat ownership/cancellation, and the existing Tauri progress event; no second retriever, HTTP client, event bus, cancellation registry, persistence format, or frontend planner mechanism was added (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:18-21`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:864-926`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:2313-2374`). Initial and per-operation progress now emit before work with stage/count-only payloads, and MCP's omitted-mode regression genuinely runs ordinary shared preparation with the lexical kill switch off and observes Fast/no planner trips (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:468-515`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:627-776`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:510-551`). Scope normalization, exact emitted capability collection, remote HTTP body cancellation/cap, sidecar line cap, and functional unmatched-transcript open/expansion are meaningful improvements, but the findings above leave R43 incompletely closed. MCP final generation remains the pre-existing duplicated path and was not extended or falsely treated as Deep parity (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:187-213`).
- Cheaper alternatives: keep the current agent and shared infrastructure. Always perform one final authoritative hydration (which already checks current membership and source revision) under a wall-clock deadline instead of adding a second membership-only fence; make the prompt builder append every section through one capped/escaped writer and return emitted IDs; map `RetrievalError::Cancelled` directly; race BuiltIn startup and repository operations with the same budget token; and either fuse each `(channel, query_slot)` as an independent RRF list or remove the slot claim. Merge requested segment IDs per meeting before one authoritative load, then append summary/note/transcript identities through the existing hydration contract. No new runtime, repository, or provider client is needed.
- Acceptance/proceed decision: **Task 4.2 cannot be accepted. Task 4.4 may not begin because it depends on 4.2. Task 4.3 remains dependency-independent, but it should not be stacked into this unaccepted shared `api/chat.rs`/retrieval worktree; only an isolated disjoint branch is reasonable until the blocker/high findings are remediated.** No release-readiness conclusion is made; inherited Sprint 3 gates remain open.
- Verification: read-only review of all current Task 4.2 production/test/doc diffs and every R43 requirement. Rejected corpus fixtures and every `retrieval_evaluation*`/debug harness were neither read nor used as evidence. `cargo test --lib retrieval::agent::tests` passed 27/27; `cargo test --lib summary::llm_client::tests` passed 19/19; `cargo test --lib mcp::server::tests` passed 6/6; focused Chat scope Vitest passed 18/18 with existing React `act(...)` warnings. These focused passes do not cover the races and adversarial identifier/multi-query semantics above and are not release evidence.
- Follow-up: remediate the remaining R43 authority, complete-prompt, cancellation/deadline, independent-fusion, and complete open/expand parity gaps, then re-review Task 4.2 before stacking Task 4.4.

### Review R45 — after Task HR-4.2.R44
- Date: 2026-09-02
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: changes-requested
- Findings (ordered by impact):
  1. **Blocker — budget expiry is still sampled before, rather than fenced through, the answer handoff.** The sole final authoritative hydration now correctly uses the budget token and re-reads current membership/source revision, and an expiry observed there fails closed (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:871-898`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/hydration.rs:220-231`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/hydration.rs:285-319`). After the one `budget.is_cancelled()` sample, however, the agent computes diagnostics, synchronously invokes the externally supplied `AnswerGeneration` progress callback, and returns success without another budget/deadline check (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:895-927`). Expiry while that callback is running can therefore publish the handoff and return evidence after the hard total budget; Chat's ownership fence cannot repair this because it fences the request token, not the agent's private budget token (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:2343-2382`). This leaves the exact R44 final-budget closure false and is directly unsafe to extend into Task 4.4's bounded Deep snapshot/today work.
  2. **High — the claimed independent planner-query RRF lists replay the original query in every new slot.** Every additional operation calls the shared retriever with the unchanged original question plus the planner query as `rewritten_query` (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1169-1192`); the service then runs original, rewritten, and original-derived core-term variants (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/service.rs:428-456`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/service.rs:715-725`). The agent relabels *all* provenance returned by that operation with the new slot (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:677-685`), and fusion now awards one contribution per slot (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:692-733`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:736-767`). Thus an item matching only the original question gains another RRF vote for each unrelated planner query, falsely appearing to have independent cross-query support and potentially crowding out the evidence the planner query found. Slot-zero/Fast ordering itself remains unchanged, but Task 4.4's query-aware broad coverage would inherit this Deep-only support inflation.
  3. **High — same-meeting open/expand merging still loses requested expansion evidence once an opened meeting has eight head segments.** An open with no target IDs loads the chronological head up to the repository cap (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/retrieval.rs:1330-1351`); later expansion segments are appended after those head rows by the accumulator (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1015-1042`), while publication blindly takes only the first eight accumulated transcript rows (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1116-1144`). For an ordinary meeting with at least eight head rows, a tail neighborhood requested by `expandEvidenceIds` is therefore merged in memory but never gets a ranked identity or reaches final hydration. The regression uses only two transcript rows, so it cannot expose the cap collision (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:664-720`). This breaks the advertised action semantics and constrains Task 4.3's neighborhood invariant and Task 4.4's bounded per-meeting coverage.
  4. **High — BuiltInAI is still declared fully capability-equivalent without enforcing the requested response-byte or wall-clock bounds.** The matrix unconditionally records full support for all seven providers (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:272-289`), but BuiltInAI passes only the token cap into the sidecar and checks the extracted text against 8 KiB only after the response has been read and parsed (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:318-341`); its actual parser/input line cap is 1 MiB (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/sidecar.rs:17-36`). Cancellation during startup/generation also awaits `shutdown()`, whose graceful wait can add three seconds beyond the 15/30-second deadline (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/client.rs:186-200`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/client.rs:233-247`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/sidecar.rs:597-612`). More seriously, `spawn()` creates the child before asynchronously installing its handle in the manager; cancellation in that interval drops the startup future and then shutdown may have no child handle to kill (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/summary_engine/sidecar.rs:392-421`). The residual sidecar-startup delay/cancellation gap therefore remains non-hermetic and unproved; the pre-cancelled test does not enter startup (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:318-341`). This is a provider-difference and orphan-work risk for Task 4.5's provider/failure/cancellation qualification, not full capability parity.
- Validated closures / structural choices: Deep orchestration remains in the Rust retrieval layer and reuses `RetrievalService`, production ranking/hydration, the existing repository, shared seven-provider request builder, cancellation tokens, and the existing Tauri ownership/event fence; no parallel retriever/client/event bus/persistence format was introduced. The complete prompt now flows through one capped writer, rejects unsafe/overlong capability IDs, escapes untrusted fields, reserves its suffix, and returns exactly emitted capabilities (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1231-1279`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1280-1321`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1333-1466`). Typed `RetrievalError::Cancelled` mapping and the SQL-load cancellation race are real (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:930-950`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/retrieval.rs:1403-1421`), as are remote HTTP body cancellation/capping (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:368-423`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:426-457`). The slot-zero RRF transformation preserves Fast behavior, forced lexical/live/ineligible scopes cannot enter Deep, MCP omission is behaviorally Fast with the kill switch off, and explicit MCP Deep remains rejected (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:692-733`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:777-864`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1715-1723`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:216-253`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:548-584`). MCP final generation remains the pre-existing duplicated uncancelled path and has not drifted into claiming Deep parity (`frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:187-213`). Existing rows/schema are untouched.
- Cheaper alternatives: keep the present thin agent and shared infrastructure. Re-check the absolute preparation deadline immediately around/after the answer-handoff callback (or make the callback budget-aware) before returning; run each planner query as that slot's original query rather than replaying the user's original variants; merge open/expand *intent* per meeting and reserve the eight-row publication budget for requested neighborhoods plus a bounded head before one final hydration; pass the requested byte cap into the existing sidecar reader and detach bounded cleanup from the caller's hard return deadline while retaining a killable child handle from spawn onward. No new runtime, provider client, repository, or event mechanism is needed.
- Final verdict / acceptance and proceed decision: **changes-requested. Task 4.2 may not be accepted. Task 4.4 may not proceed because it depends on 4.2. Task 4.3 is dependency-independent and may proceed only in an isolated/disjoint branch or worktree; it should not be stacked into this still-unaccepted shared `api/chat.rs`/retrieval worktree.** No release conclusion is made.
- Verification: read-only review of the actual current Task 4.2/R43/R44 production, focused-test, and documentation diff. Rejected corpus fixtures and `retrieval_evaluation*`/debug harnesses were neither read nor used as evidence. `cargo test --lib retrieval::agent::tests` passed 36/36; `cargo test --lib summary::llm_client::tests` passed 20/20; `cargo test --lib api::chat::tests` passed 50/50; `cargo test --lib mcp::server::tests` passed 6/6; `cargo check` passed with the existing unused-`Sidebar` warning; frontend typecheck and focused Chat scope Vitest passed 18/18 with existing React `act(...)` warnings; `git diff --check` passed with the recorded TypeScript line-ending warning. `cargo fmt --check` could not complete because unrelated rejected `corpus_validation_v10` fixture files contain existing trailing whitespace; those fixtures were not used as review evidence.
- Follow-up: close the final budget-to-handoff fence, planner-slot query attribution, capped open/expand union, and real BuiltInAI byte/deadline/startup capability gaps, then re-review Task 4.2 before Task 4.4.

### Review R46 — final acceptance after Task HR-4.2.R45
- Date: 2026-09-02
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: approve
- Findings (ordered by impact): **clean — no blocker, high, or medium structural findings.** R45's four closures are present in production code and discriminative focused tests: the sole final hydration authority is followed by wall-clock/token checks both before and after the synchronous answer-generation callback, with parent cancellation independently re-fenced, so neither context nor sources can leave Deep after expiry/cancellation (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:877-945`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:1698-1757`); each planner query is now the additional request's actual `original_query`, `rewritten_query` is absent, and every `folder:"..."` operator is removed before the fixed authoritative scope reaches the shared retriever, preventing scope syntax from changing authority or replaying the user's original variants into the new slot (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:650-690`, `:1234-1265`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/fts.rs:9-10`, `:94-130`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:1069-1130`); same-meeting loads retain the union while explicit expansion-returned segment IDs are recorded and ordered ahead of open/head rows under the unchanged eight-segment publication cap (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1033-1092`, `:1094-1209`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:723-782`); and BuiltInAI truthfully fails the planner's all-bounds capability check before its sidecar branch while ordinary Chat continues through the separate existing `generate_summary` BuiltInAI path (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:99-134`, `:272-355`, `:935-988`).
- Safety/parity re-audit: Task 4.2/R40/R41 properties remain intact. Deep stays in the Rust retrieval layer and reuses `RetrievalService`, ranking, authoritative hydration/repository loading, and the shared seven-provider client (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:206-268`, `:460-518`, `:644-866`). Strict whole-payload parsing, numeric/action limits, exact prompt-emitted capability sets, escaped/capped untrusted prompt content, and per-round card/evidence allow-lists remain enforced (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:108-146`, `:355-436`, `:1303-1536`). Final scope/revision authority remains one fail-closed hydration; independent provenance-slot RRF fusion and final retained-source hydration parity remain shared rather than parallel paths (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:877-905`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/ranking.rs:692-767`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/hydration.rs:220-231`, `:258-319`). Per-call/total watchdog cancellation and the Chat ownership event fence still prevent stale/replaced/cancelled publication; only stage identity/counts cross Tauri (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:443-477`, `:547-603`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:2343-2382`). Forced lexical/live/ineligible paths resolve to Fast before constructing the planner, Fast emits no Deep progress, MCP omission remains behaviorally Fast with explicit Deep rejected, and MCP's duplicated final generation has not drifted into Deep behavior (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:777-864`, `:920-954`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:187-253`, `:548-584`). No schema/migration or existing-row compatibility change is part of Task 4.2.
- Cheaper alternatives: none warranted. The implementation already uses the smallest correct shape: one thin Rust policy agent over existing retrieval/provider/hydration/event infrastructure. Do not add a frontend planner, second retriever/client, event bus, cancellation registry, or persistence format.
- Residual risks: BuiltInAI Deep planning is intentionally unavailable and falls back to current Fast evidence until its sidecar can enforce a true mid-read byte cap and hard startup/generation/cleanup deadline; ordinary BuiltInAI Chat remains available. MCP final answer generation is still the pre-existing duplicated call without an internal cancellation token and must be kept in parity when its later MCP deadline task changes it (`frontend/src-tauri/src/summary/llm_client.rs:272-355`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs:187-213`). Measured Deep p50/p95, production provider/quality evidence, all-scope hardening, and deleted-source qualification remain Task 4.5/release gates, not claims of this acceptance (`docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md:469-524`; `docs/notes-chat-improvement-execution.md:254`).
- Acceptance/proceed decision: **Task 4.2 is accepted. Tasks 4.3 and 4.4 may start in the documented dependency order.** This is an implementation acceptance only; Sprint 3 inherited corpus, production quality/provider-answer, native Windows/R13 hermetic-session, and exact-head Actions gates remain open, and no release conclusion is made.

Frontend typecheck passed and focused Chat scope Vitest passed 18/18 with existing React `act(...)` warnings; `git diff --check` passed with the recorded TypeScript CRLF warning. The untrusted/rejected corpus, every `retrieval_evaluation*` harness, and debug fixtures were neither read nor run and are not evidence for this review.
- Follow-up tasks created: none. Existing Tasks 4.3, 4.4, and 4.5 retain their documented scope and gates.
